#122871 7 2005-12-14T12:32:10Z ska

#122881 10 2005-12-14T13:32:13Z ska
No Way. I cannot believe it I did it again. I even changed my jump button to shift to prevent skipping the replay. It doesn't work in debug mode I've tried. But If you guys could try to do it you'll know how to post it on here. I'll explain how to do this as well as possible.
1. Set your jump key to shift.
2. Jump up the left slope as fast as you can
3. Try to land your last jump at the key in top-left corner.
4. Jump in the corner. Hopefully you will die in the process.
5. Now press 'R' to view the replay you will survive where you died before and you will even be able to control the last three seconds of the demo. I'll bet that's how the 0th on this level was accomplished too.

Here is quote from school rules. It states:
MOBILE PHONES
Students are reminded that the use of mobile phones is prohibited during class time, chapel services, assemblies, tutorials, in the library, drama productions, music performances and other official school functions. Mobile phones must be left in a locked locker between 8:45am and 3:30pm. Inappropriate use of mobile phones will result in the phone being held by a Head of the Senior School.

The School does not except responsibility for the loss or theft of mobile phones carried for private use.
----------------------------------------------
OK, now here is my dilemma. It started last term. It was the last day of term. It was say 5-10 minutes before school started. As always, my phone was on silent and I often use it to set silent reminders to help me avoid getting onto trouble in thte first place. Anyway, this kid at school wants to have a look at my phone. And without me realising he turns my phone off silent!

Of course while I'm distracted talking to my mate he casually slips it in my blazer pocket. Not my pants pocket where i usually place it. So when I take my blazer off after a assembly about cane toads, I take my blazer off and lock it in my locker.

So as the day goes on, it gradually becomes colder. I put my blazer back on; completely unaware that my phone was in the side pocket of my blazer

During English we were watching a movie when my phone rings. Madly slapping my pocket before I eventually get it to hang up. But the damage was done.

Now, the aftermath after telling the secretary and the principal my predicament, they won't have a bar of it. Oh it should be locked in my locker. well I'm sorry but i assumed it was! And here's the kicker: The other kid, who was stupid enough to not turn caller ID off was tracked. Yet somehow, after telling these two 'authority' people he gets off scot free. I am not the first, nor will I be the last.

About two months ago another kid was looking for his phone. It was stolen by a classmate, placed in his sports bag. The called it. He go a Friday Night Detention. He wasn't even in the room!
I stood up for him, because he didn't deserve it.
And I don't want the same thing to happen to anyone else. [B]It's just not fair!

Now, I want your opinions please.

#225063 31 2006-09-07T06:13:38Z ska
Quote: Ciph3rzer0, September 05, 2006 06:05 am
I'm thinking right now of a box and whisker chart. Anyone remember those? It was like, a line graph of sorts with two boxes, one extending from the [average or median] to the lower 25% limit and one the other way. Then there were lines to the dots at the extremes. Well, whatever, we never really used it, they just taught it to us because it was on the PSSA's anyways, my point is that it was used to exclude obvious extreme's, numbers that may have shown up but do not accuratly describe the variable it represent's. I always wondered if a similar method could be implicated in rating systems; just filter out the extreme's. And then you could even go as far as to keep a record of that. Such as a "creditablitly" score. So someone who always votes too low will lose creditability and his/her vote will count for less. *shrug* just my thoughts, unfortunatly I dont have a website where people rate stuff to test it on.

I think this a really good suggestion. I have heard something similar been suggested but this version sounds good. It's almost like now you have to strive for top rated but make sure you get someone to bump it off, by giving it a slightly lower rating as to avoid your map going to 4. Now I have had say 5 maps that I can remember that have been in the top 10. Every single one of those maps had gone down to 4.0, It's disgraceful.

#11834 1 2006-10-19T22:51:50Z ska
Version 1.0 released: 20th of October, 2006
Smart Thwumps, Hang bars, (Ninja can hang on blue bars)
Special Mario enemy in secret level!
Animated, moving characters!
Menu --> Game --> Password, enter password for bonus level!
Can't walljump... yet. Jump sensititvity isn't great and level transition will be smoother in future versions. Guass turrets will fire lasers and I'll see if I can make N hit the ground running. I'll try to incorporate as much as I can in the next version. We'll see...
Pretty good for a 10 year old program.
No code, so it take 5 minutes to learn and a life-time to master. I'm still learning
Took me ages to make and I tried to implement some ideas that people have come up with to see how they would work in the game.
Have Fun!

Edit: I'm Sorry. There will be no future versions as I am no longer going to be here.

The Twist!
An 'intruder' has secretly demoed a selected map every round and has now officially entered the Elimination contest as an intruder. This means that he must finish the round in the top 2 to continue on to round 3. If the intruder fails to do this, he will be eliminated for good and will replace mysterypower as the 16th or last place getter. If he succeeds to achieve a top 2 placing in group 1 he can continue as a normal competitor. It is the duty of lord_day, Rocket_Thumped, uNcoNditioNal and Hendor to make sure this doesn't happen. If the intruder succeeds, the last (5th) place getter will be eliminated as usual. The winner (including the intruder) will get immunity for each group. The intruder's identity will be revealed officially when round three commences, although some of you may already have guessed who the intruder is...

#13500 1 2007-01-13T09:38:27Z ska
To find this out, use nhigh to find out your overall top-20 scores divided by the total 0th's you have this will give you your 'strike rate'. e.g. I have 17 top 20 scores and one 0th that means I have a strike rate of 17. L3X for example has 470 top 20 scores and 41 0th's. 470 divided by 41 = 11.46.

What's your strike rate if you have one that is...

#325972 4 2007-05-31T11:05:22Z ska
Let me try...

If you are trying to make the level you should read the read me. If you you don't have it because someone gave it to you without it try this.

1. Press the ~ button, usually located near the top left of your keyboard
2. press the 'R' key
3. Press the 'P' key
4. Make sure caps lock is switched ON or you will play in Frame By Frame Mode.

Did this answer your question? If not, read on.

If you mean reset all the data you will need to erase your .sol file. This can be done in two ways.
1. Re-image your computer.
2. Run a search for .sol as the file name, assuming you are running windows, make sure you check 'looking in hidden files and folders' and 'system files' otherwise you probably won't find it.
a. Select the file that says: n_v14b_userdata.sol
b. Delete it.

I suggest doing the latter but...

WARNING! This will permanently erase all N data and preferences, If you are having problems unlocking a certain episode. Try beating the previous episode in the column with practice mode switched OFF in the configure menu.

Did this help? If not, fend for yourself!!

On the 12th of Septmeber, I submitted my very first map to NUMA. (Although I had made others but didn't know how to save them, lol).

Although I have been playing since 1.3 days in 2004. I clearly remember one ridiculously boring French class at which I sucked at Frence so it was boring! A friend put this game called N on his 64MB (OMG so much memory!!! I thought... seriously though a dollar per megabyte!?)

Anyway, I remember being so shit when I first played. 0-0 was like hey look at this I am cheating I am 'wall jumping' up the wall!!! Look at me go WOOOO!!! Then i learnt if you were in narrow columns you could tap shift (as it were) repeatedly to gather extra speed and height. I clocked 1.3 (much to my friends' amazement) then N v1.4 came out and after a couple of months chipping away slowly, but surely I was really popular with people who couldn't beat a perticular level. "Hey, Schultzy beat this level" "okay! "

The history teacher would be like, "Hey, Ben watchya doing?" I'd be like, "Just helping Brotchie download his work.."
"Okay" he said.

After a while I got hooked playing N. I bludged alot of schoolwork, made a few maps which I got my mates to play and got a few highscores.

Then you know the rest. My votes got resetted. I was banned. I made 'ska' and the rest is history...

I am only really starting to knuckle down on school work now and can't wait till athetics day . It's all coming along well. Thanks for the awesome memories

Ben Schultz

#17601 1 2007-10-19T11:11:13Z ska
Today you should back up your sol file to a memory stick, iPod, CD etc. (something other than your computer in case of accidental progress deletion.) Make sure you copy not cut the .sol file. If you don't know how to locate your .sol file simply search for .sol, making sure you've checked hidden files and folders or alternatively:
Quote
Edrial=both Macs and Windows use the same .sol file. their formats are no different and are compatible.

Windows .sol location: C:\Documents and Settings\(Username)\Application Data\Macromedia\Flash Player\#Shared Objects\ usually a one way street from there...
*Note: Application Data will probably be a hidden folder

not sure if this is exact because i don't have a Mac....
Mac .sol location: Home > Library > Peferences > Macromedia > Flash Player > #Shared Objects\ usually a one way street from there...


just put the .sol in that location. replace the current .sol if necessary. always keep a back-up.
Put this in high scores because I know most of the people who have this problem are high scorers. OK, Good Luck and make sure you back-up regularly, it might be a pain in the ass to do it now but imagine the excruciating pain in the ass if you don't and you lose it all! This has been a public service announcement.

I think I figured out the problem! There was this glitch in N200 itself and this is what happened (randomly a few times)

Say I played episode 192 and my score was really bad, I'd go back later and play it again and SOMETIMES I would get a message saying "New Personal Best" when actually my score was not as good, of course whenever I saw this I quickly recovered my highscore from the highscores (because obviously you can't submit a score that is lower than your highscore!) But I might not have noticed, especially the first few times, so I think I actually believe unreality's site MIGHT be more accurate.. Has anyone else stumbled upon this too?

You can�t simply sign up to enter this competition.
